# Boxes test cleanup ledger

Audit scope: **83 baseline files, 729 named declarations** (parameterized declarations are one row). Dispositions: **CONSOLIDATE 7**, **DELETE 22**, **KEEP 675**, **OPTIMIZE 7**, **REWRITE 18**. Net test LOC: **-380**. One metadata-only test file is removed.

Every row records a contract/failure and executable oracle. The six-bar rationale is shared by its file section to avoid repeating protocol/schema citations in every row. Retained tests use the lowest seam that distinguishes their listed failure; service integration, native wire interoperability, and provider translation are separate ownership boundaries. Imported boundary constants position scenarios; expected outcomes must still be literal states/data/side effects.

## Implemented changes

- Removed export inventories, repeated unsupported-adapter calls, duplicate shared retry/cancellation executions, private-shell-variable/source-shape assertions, and exact diagnostic copy assertions.
- Replaced private parser coverage with public PUT validation/persistence, and Blacksmith credential-key inventory with real setup-to-adapter authentication.
- Repaired durability assertions: observes actual file/directory fsync order and on-disk state. Repaired an assertion-free security negative with no-spawn assertion and own-box positive control.
- Removed BoxConfigWatches.watched(), made the internal parser/port helpers, stage-variant constant/type, Google fingerprint and six Vast.ai implementation constants/helpers private after repository-wide caller search. Their actual production logic remains.
- Removed optional shellcheck test subprocesses, preserved native shell parsing and actual bootstrap executions, removed duplicate parser inputs, and bypassed only test sandbox retry backoffs.
- Added teardown for three sync-store and four MCP-bridge temp directories; reduced aborted/capped search fixtures. No snapshots, provider fixtures, shared dependency or manifest were orphaned by these changes.

## Measurement and verification

The first baseline run was interrupted before a complete total; root is collecting a frozen isolated baseline and owns the final overall comparison. The original runner used package serialization and Node test concurrency 4. Individual original boxes samples are listed below; they are not a sum of file/suite wall time. Files with identical test titles cannot be assigned timings unambiguously from the spec reporter. No final speedup or CI savings are claimed before comparable final measurement.

Original measured largest case: bootstrap POSIX parse/shellcheck **13,340.231 ms**. Real wget retry **2,680.868 ms**, redirect retry **2,655.372 ms**, staged retry/restart **2,532.457 ms**. These are the directly changed bottlenecks. Native Chromium reconnect/output-overrun (~6 s each), Go executable upgrade (~5 s), real desktop/window/audio, SSH auth, and Git watcher scenarios intentionally remain: they prove external runtime interoperability and lifecycle outcomes that mocks do not.

Implementation-stage checks: `git diff --check -- apps/daemon/src/boxes` passed. No tests, typechecks or builds were run by this owner before root grants the scheduled validation slot. Root owns the full gates. Focused target: every changed boxes `.test.ts`, plus `src/boxes/sync/config-sync.test.ts` (changed alongside watch seam). PowerShell execution is unavailable here; only native format/path bytes are checked for that output. No production daemon was started.

## `apps/daemon/src/boxes/bootstrap/bootstrap.test.ts`

1. **Independent contract:** Design §§5–6, 8.2; BoxBootstrap/BootstrapInput and native cloud-init/container/POSIX/PowerShell distribution protocols.
2. **Recognizable regression:** each row names the distinct failed operation/state/security property; it must fail the listed observable oracle.
3. **Independent oracle:** literal outputs, known native-format fixtures, specified state transitions, or separately observed filesystem/process/network artifacts shown below; no coverage percentage or production-inventory equality is a retention reason.
4. **Stable seam:** buildBootstrap serialization and actually executed generated scripts.
5. **Refactor resistance:** retained assertions concern accepted inputs, outputs, native protocol bytes or visible side effects; removed private variable/export/call-shape/prose assertions are identified individually.
6. **Unique protection:** lifecycle/provider tests pass bootstrap through; only these cases execute downloads, retry/refusal/security and serialized native inputs.
**Production callers:** provider lifecycle and hub bootstrap endpoints. **Risk:** retained native security/persistence/lifecycle scenarios are high consequence; deletions are restricted to the failed bar and named surviving owner. **Validation:** package-directory `TSX_DISABLE_CACHE=1 node --import tsx --test src/boxes/bootstrap/bootstrap.test.ts`; scheduled execution is recorded separately, never inferred from this command.

| Baseline test name | Disposition | Contract, unique failure, and oracle / surviving owner |
|---|---|---|
| validates its input before anything reaches a script | KEEP | Protects validates its input before anything reaches a script. Distinct scenario at this file’s stated seam; failure is the wrong caller-visible outcome rather than a collaborator invocation inventory. Baseline oracle evidence: `assert.throws(() => buildBootstrap(input({ boxId: "x" })), /box id/); assert.throws(() => buildBootstrap(input({ bootstrapToken: "short" })), /token/); assert.throws(() => buildBootstrap(input({ bootstrapToken: \`${TOKEN}'; rm -rf /\` })), /token/)`. |
| secrets appear only where a script assigns or writes them, never in argv | REWRITE | Replaced private shell-variable greps with parsed cloud-init secret records (0600), no literal secret in container argv/script, exact public environment keys and native PowerShell header/platform path bytes. POSIX real execution owns argv/log secrecy. No PowerShell execution claim. Baseline oracle evidence: `assert.deepEqual(posixLines, [\`T='${TOKEN}'\`, \`K='${TS_KEY}'\`]); assert.match(boot.posixScript, /^set \+x$/m); assert.ok(boot.containerCommand.every((arg) => !arg.includes(TOKEN) && !arg.includes(TS_KEY)))`. |
| the staged bootstrap: secrets only in the first stage's assignments; the second stage is generic | DELETE | Fails stable-seam/refactor-resistance/unique-protection bars: greps private shell variable names and compares a generic generator to itself. Real staged execution checks bearer auth, argv secrecy, key-file cleanup, restart cache and refused-token behavior. |
| containers use userspace tailscaled and hand orq-agent its proxy | REWRITE | Retains native --tun=userspace-networking flag and ORQ_PROXY environment key plus Tailscale installer URL; removes assertions naming private PX/B shell variables and duplicated launch shape. Other bootstrap executions cover actual starts. Baseline oracle evidence: `assert.match(script, /--tun=userspace-networking/); assert.match(script, /ORQ_PROXY=\$PX/); assert.doesNotMatch(script, /install-service/)`. |
| the POSIX scripts parse under dash, bash and busybox sh, and pass shellcheck | OPTIMIZE | Keeps native shell grammar checks. Removes optional shellcheck subprocess/style assertions: no warning-free contract exists in the spec or repository gates, and it silently disappeared on hosts without that executable. Bootstrap also deduplicates identical generated scripts before native parser runs. Baseline oracle evidence: `assert.ok(shells.length > 0, "at least one POSIX shell to check with"); assert.equal(result.status, 0, \`${shell.join(" ")}: ${result.stderr}\`); assert.equal(result.status, 0, \`shellcheck:\n${result.stdout}\`)`. |
| posixScript downloads with the bearer token via curl, joins the tailnet by key file, and execs orq-agent | KEEP | Protects posixScript downloads with the bearer token via curl, joins the tailnet by key file, and execs orq-agent. Distinct scenario at this file’s stated seam; failure is the wrong caller-visible outcome rather than a collaborator invocation inventory. Baseline oracle evidence: `assert.equal(code, 0, output); assertAgentStarted(box, daemon, output); assert.equal(daemon.requests.length, 1)`. |
| posixScript falls back to GNU wget and retries a failing daemon | OPTIMIZE | Real shell, real loopback HTTP and bearer/redirect/restart assertions stay; a sandbox PATH sleep shim removes backoff waiting because elapsed retry time is not the contract. Exact requested retry sequences and final artifacts still prove behavior. Baseline oracle evidence: `assert.equal(code, 0, output); assert.match(output, /download failed \(HTTP 503\); retrying/); assert.equal(daemon.requests.length, 2)`. |
| the wget fallback never follows a redirect with the bearer token | OPTIMIZE | Real shell, real loopback HTTP and bearer/redirect/restart assertions stay; a sandbox PATH sleep shim removes backoff waiting because elapsed retry time is not the contract. Exact requested retry sequences and final artifacts still prove behavior. Baseline oracle evidence: `assert.equal(code, 0, output); assert.match(output, /download failed \(HTTP 302\); retrying/); assert.deepEqual( daemon.requests.map((request) => request.path.replace(/linux-(x64\|arm64)(-musl)?$/, "linux")), ["/api/boxes/agent/linux", "/api/boxes/agent/linux"] )`. |
| containerCommand takes its secrets from env, unsets them and stays in the foreground | KEEP | Protects containerCommand takes its secrets from env, unsets them and stays in the foreground. Distinct scenario at this file’s stated seam; failure is the wrong caller-visible outcome rather than a collaborator invocation inventory. Baseline oracle evidence: `assert.equal(code, 0, output); assertAgentStarted(box, daemon, output); assert.doesNotMatch(agentEnv, /^ORQ_BOOTSTRAP_TOKEN=/m)`. |
| the cloud-init script reads the token file written by write_files | KEEP | Protects the cloud-init script reads the token file written by write_files. Distinct scenario at this file’s stated seam; failure is the wrong caller-visible outcome rather than a collaborator invocation inventory. Baseline oracle evidence: `assert.equal(code, 0, output); assertAgentStarted(box, daemon, output)`. |
| a refused token stops the bootstrap without printing it | KEEP | Protects a refused token stops the bootstrap without printing it. Distinct scenario at this file’s stated seam; failure is the wrong caller-visible outcome rather than a collaborator invocation inventory. Baseline oracle evidence: `assert.equal(code, 1); assert.match(output, /refused the bootstrap token \(HTTP 401\)/); assert.ok(!output.includes("obt_wrong-token"))`. |
| stagedPosixScript joins the tailnet, fetches the second stage with the bearer token, and that starts orq-agent | OPTIMIZE | Real shell, real loopback HTTP and bearer/redirect/restart assertions stay; a sandbox PATH sleep shim removes backoff waiting because elapsed retry time is not the contract. Exact requested retry sequences and final artifacts still prove behavior. Baseline oracle evidence: `assert.equal(code, 0, output); assert.match(output, /download failed \(HTTP 502\); retrying/); assertAgentStarted(box, daemon, output)`. |

Distinct scenario at this file’s stated seam; failure is the wrong caller-visible outcome rather than a collaborator invocation inventory. Baseline oracle evidence: `assert.equal(code, 1); assert.match(output, /refused the bootstrap token \(HTTP 401\)/); assert.ok(!output.includes(TOKEN))`. |

6. **Unique protection:** credential parsing owns grant privacy; broker cases own scheduling/holder/cooldown races; real service cases own integration and router holder classification.

| Baseline test name | Disposition | Contract, unique failure, and oracle / surviving owner |
|---|---|---|
| a fresh token is handed out as is, with only the access token and non-secret facts | KEEP | Protects a fresh token is handed out as is, with only the access token and non-secret facts. Distinct scenario at this file’s stated seam; failure is the wrong caller-visible outcome rather than a collaborator invocation inventory. Baseline oracle evidence: `assert.deepEqual(claude, { accessToken: "claude-access-A", expiresAt: NOW + 5 * 60 * MIN, extra: { subscriptionType: "max", rateLimitTier: "default_claude_max_20x", scopes: "user:inference user:profile user:sessions:claude_code" } }); assert.deepEqual(Object.keys(codex).sort(), ["accessToken", "expiresAt", "extra"]); assert.deepEqual(codex.extra, { chatgptAccountId: "ws-1" }, "tokens.account_id wins over the claim")`. |
| no refresh token ever leaves the broker, before or after a refresh | DELETE | Fails unique protection: grantFromCredentials is the single field-filter owner; its three-provider secret cases, fresh-grant broker case, and real refresh-service case retain pre/post-refresh privacy without repeating three mocked refreshes. |
| a token below minValidSeconds is refreshed when no host-local session holds the account | KEEP | Protects a token below minValidSeconds is refreshed when no host-local session holds the account. Distinct scenario at this file’s stated seam; failure is the wrong caller-visible outcome rather than a collaborator invocation inventory. Baseline oracle evidence: `assert.equal(grant.accessToken, "claude-access-NEW"); assert.deepEqual(w.refreshCalls, [{ agent: "claude", id: CLAUDE_ID, allowHeld: false }]); assert.equal(short.accessToken, "claude-access-SHORT")`. |
| box sessions are not refreshers: an account only box chats use is refreshed on demand | DELETE | Fails independent oracle/unique protection: the mock hostLocal set is already empty, so the test assumes the router classification it claims to prove. Real ChatAwareSessionManager and AgentAccountsService cases in refresh-scheduling.test.ts own it. |
| concurrent requests for one account share one refresh | KEEP | Protects concurrent requests for one account share one refresh. Baseline oracle evidence: `assert.equal(w.refreshCalls.length, 1, "one refresh for three requests"); assert.deepEqual(new Set(grants.map((g) => g.accessToken)), new Set(["grok-access-NEW"]))`. |
| a host-local Codex holder: the request waits for that CLI's rewrite and never refreshes | KEEP | Protects a host-local Codex holder: the request waits for that CLI's rewrite and never refreshes. Distinct scenario at this file’s stated seam; failure is the wrong caller-visible outcome rather than a collaborator invocation inventory. Baseline oracle evidence: `assert.equal(settled, false, "still waiting"); assert.equal(settled, false); assert.equal(grant.expiresAt, fresh * 1000)`. |
| a host-local holder whose session ends: the daemon refreshes then | KEEP | Protects a host-local holder whose session ends: the daemon refreshes then. Distinct scenario at this file’s stated seam; failure is the wrong caller-visible outcome rather than a collaborator invocation inventory. Baseline oracle evidence: `assert.equal(w.refreshCalls.length, 0); assert.equal(grant.accessToken, "grok-access-NEW"); assert.deepEqual(w.refreshCalls, [{ agent: "grok", id: GROK_ID, allowHeld: false }])`. |
| a wait ends with TOKEN_WAIT_TIMEOUT when the request's signal aborts | KEEP | Protects a wait ends with TOKEN_WAIT_TIMEOUT when the request's signal aborts. Distinct scenario at this file’s stated seam; failure is the wrong caller-visible outcome rather than a collaborator invocation inventory. Baseline oracle evidence: `rejectsWith(pending, "TOKEN_WAIT_TIMEOUT", 504); assert.equal(w.files.watcherCount(codexPath), 0); assert.equal(w.refreshCalls.length, 0)`. |
| a host-local Claude holder: refreshed directly above the floor, waited for below it or while its CLI holds the refresh lock | KEEP | Protects a host-local Claude holder: refreshed directly above the floor, waited for below it or while its CLI holds the refresh lock. Distinct scenario at this file’s stated seam; failure is the wrong caller-visible outcome rather than a collaborator invocation inventory. Baseline oracle evidence: `assert.equal(direct.accessToken, "claude-access-NEW"); assert.deepEqual(w.refreshCalls, [{ agent: "claude", id: CLAUDE_ID, allowHeld: true }]); assert.equal(w.refreshCalls.length, 0)`. |

"TOKEN_NEEDS_REAUTH" : "TOKEN_UNAVAILABLE", i === 0 ? 409 : 503 )`. |
| no renewal possible (no refresh token, service unreachable): a still-usable token is handed out, never a rejected or nearly spent one | KEEP | Protects no renewal possible (no refresh token, service unreachable): a still-usable token is handed out, never a rejected or nearly spent one. Removes exact step labels/index, imported-constant self comparisons and template self-equality. Baseline oracle evidence: `assert.equal(workflow["run-name"], "orq-${{ inputs.box_id }}"); assert.deepEqual(Object.keys(workflow.on.workflow_dispatch.inputs), ["box_id", "lifetime_minutes", "daemon_url", "runner"]); assert.deepEqual(workflow.on.workflow_dispatch.inputs.runner!.options, [...BLACKSMITH_RUNNER_LABELS])`. |
| the optional Tailscale step uses workload identity, the box tag and hostname | REWRITE | Retains action inputs/tag/hostname and join-before-authenticated-download ordering, instead of fixed incidental step index 1. Baseline oracle evidence: `assert.deepEqual(step.with, { "oauth-client-id": "kAbc123CNTRL", audience: "api.tailscale.com/kAbc123CNTRL", tags: "tag:orq-box", hostname: "orq-${{ inputs.box_id }}" }); assert.equal(steps.indexOf(step), 1, "joins before the download"); assert.throws(() => blacksmithWorkflowTemplate({ tailscale: { oauthClientId: "x\ny", audience: "a" } }), /Invalid/)`. |
| the step scripts pass bash -n and shellcheck | OPTIMIZE | Keeps native shell grammar checks. Removes optional shellcheck subprocess/style assertions: no warning-free contract exists in the spec or repository gates, and it silently disappeared on hosts without that executable. Bootstrap also deduplicates identical generated scripts before native parser runs. Baseline oracle evidence: `assert.equal(syntax.status, 0, syntax.stderr); assert.equal(lint.status, 0, lint.stdout)`. |
| the input check rejects values that could break out of the run | KEEP | Protects the input check rejects values that could break out of the run. Distinct scenario at this file’s stated seam; failure is the wrong caller-visible outcome rather than a collaborator invocation inventory. Baseline oracle evidence: `assert.equal(check(good), 0); assert.equal(check({ ...good, BOX_ID: "x; curl evil" }), 1); assert.equal(check({ ...good, DAEMON_URL: "http://orq.example.com" }), 1)`. |
| the download step gets an OIDC token for the daemon origin and sends it as GitHubOIDC | KEEP | Protects the download step gets an OIDC token for the daemon origin and sends it as GitHubOIDC. Distinct scenario at this file’s stated seam; failure is the wrong caller-visible outcome rather than a collaborator invocation inventory. Baseline oracle evidence: `assert.equal(code, 0, output); assert.ok(!output.includes("SECRET") && !output.includes("aaa.bbb.ccc"), "no token in the step log"); assert.equal(oidc.searchParams.get("audience"), origin, "audience = the daemon origin, without the base path")`. |

## `apps/daemon/src/boxes/remote/gui/scripts.test.ts`

1. **Independent contract:** Design §§7, 8.4–8.9; BoxConnection contracts; public file/git/session/desktop/browser/workflow API and filesystem containment rules.
2. **Recognizable regression:** each row names the distinct failed operation/state/security property; it must fail the listed observable oracle.
3. **Independent oracle:** literal outputs, known native-format fixtures, specified state transitions, or separately observed filesystem/process/network artifacts shown below; no coverage percentage or production-inventory equality is a retention reason.
4. **Stable seam:** public managers/routes/operations over box link, real filesystem/processes/servers where required.
5. **Refactor resistance:** retained assertions concern accepted inputs, outputs, native protocol bytes or visible side effects; removed private variable/export/call-shape/prose assertions are identified individually.
6. **Unique protection:** box-specific routing, path translation, link-drop behavior and host-only secret isolation are absent from ordinary host owners; protocol framing is separately owned by client/hub.
**Production callers:** daemon frontend routes, BrowserManager/DesktopManager, GitService, workflow runner and MCP tools. **Risk:** retained native security/persistence/lifecycle scenarios are high consequence; deletions are restricted to the failed bar and named surviving owner. **Validation:** package-directory `TSX_DISABLE_CACHE=1 node --import tsx --test src/boxes/remote/gui/scripts.test.ts`; scheduled execution is recorded separately, never inferred from this command.

| Baseline test name | Disposition | Contract, unique failure, and oracle / surviving owner |
|---|---|---|
| generated scripts are valid POSIX sh for every package manager | KEEP | Protects generated scripts are valid POSIX sh for every package manager. Distinct scenario at this file’s stated seam; failure is the wrong caller-visible outcome rather than a collaborator invocation inventory. Baseline oracle evidence: `Native /bin/sh -n must accept each generated install/start script.`. |
| apt scripts are non-interactive, skip recommends and wait for the dpkg lock | CONSOLIDATE | Deleted source/regex helper-spelling checks. Real shell execution in the existing stale-list install case now observes exported noninteractive env, the dpkg lock option and recovery command, alongside existing no-recommends argv assertions. Baseline oracle evidence: `assert.match(script, /DEBIAN_FRONTEND=noninteractive/); assert.match(script, /--no-install-recommends/); assert.match(script, /DPkg::Lock::Timeout=600/)`. |
| the Chrome install uses Google's signed repository and pins the key's fingerprint | REWRITE | Keeps literal Google signing/repository URLs, HTTPS-only flag, package and distro keyring paths plus RPM gpgcheck. Removes the tautological production-fingerprint-constant comparison and shell-control-flow source shape; fingerprint is private and no security verification claim is made by that removed assertion. Baseline oracle evidence: `assert.match(apt, /https:\/\/dl\.google\.com\/linux\/linux_signing_key\.pub/); assert.ok(apt.includes(GOOGLE_SIGNING_KEY_FINGERPRINT)); assert.match(apt, /signed-by=%s\] %s stable main/)`. |
| apt: stale lists are refreshed and the install retried; a missing optional package is skipped alone | CONSOLIDATE | Surviving owner executes generated POSIX shell against command stand-ins and inspects native argv/env and success despite an optional package failure; catches omitted flags/recovery commands without naming private shell helpers. Baseline oracle evidence: `assert.equal(code, 0, out); assert.match(calls, /apt-get .* update/); assert.match(calls, /apt-get .*install --no-install-recommends tigervnc-standalone-server openbox$/m)`. |
| apt: a required package that cannot be installed fails the script with exit 3 | KEEP | Protects apt: a required package that cannot be installed fails the script with exit 3. Distinct scenario at this file’s stated seam; failure is the wrong caller-visible outcome rather than a collaborator invocation inventory. Baseline oracle evidence: `assert.equal(code, EXIT_REQUIRED_FAILED); assert.match(out, /Could not install the desktop packages\./); assert.doesNotMatch(out, /Desktop packages are installed/)`. |
| dnf: an optional package falls back to its alternative name | KEEP | Protects dnf: an optional package falls back to its alternative name. Distinct scenario at this file’s stated seam; failure is the wrong caller-visible outcome rather than a collaborator invocation inventory. Baseline oracle evidence: `assert.equal(code, 0, out); assert.match(calls, /dnf install -y --setopt=install_weak_deps=False ffmpeg-free$/m); assert.doesNotMatch(out, /Skipped/)`. |
| Chrome start check: sandbox first for a normal user, --no-sandbox when it fails | KEEP | Protects chrome start check: sandbox first for a normal user, --no-sandbox when it fails. Distinct scenario at this file’s stated seam; failure is the wrong caller-visible outcome rather than a collaborator invocation inventory. Baseline oracle evidence: `assert.equal(parsed.sandbox, null, "root never tries the sandbox"); assert.equal(parsed.sandbox, false); assert.ok(parsed.log.some((line) => line.includes("No usable sandbox")), withSandbox.out)`. |
| parseChromeStart reads both attempts and their logs | KEEP | Protects parseChromeStart reads both attempts and their logs. Distinct scenario at this file’s stated seam; failure is the wrong caller-visible outcome rather than a collaborator invocation inventory. Baseline oracle evidence: `assert.deepEqual(parseChromeStart("sandbox=ok\n"), { sandbox: true, noSandbox: null, log: [] }); assert.deepEqual(parseChromeStart("sandbox=fail\nsandbox-log: boom\nnosandbox=fail\nnosandbox-log: bang\nnoise\n"), { sandbox: false, noSandbox: false, log: ["sandbox-log: boom", "nosandbox-log: bang"] })`. |

6. **Unique protection:** providers own native API translation; these cases uniquely own billable record ordering, multi-appdir ownership, resume/destroy decisions, tolerant writes and public status/cost.
**Production callers:** daemon wiring, project/session routing, temporary tools and HTTP clients. **Risk:** retained native security/persistence/lifecycle scenarios are high consequence; deletions are restricted to the failed bar and named surviving owner. **Validation:** package-directory `TSX_DISABLE_CACHE=1 node --import tsx --test src/boxes/service/mcp-run-in-box.test.ts`; scheduled execution is recorded separately, never inferred from this command.

| Baseline test name | Disposition | Contract, unique failure, and oracle / surviving owner |
|---|---|---|
| GET lists every installed agent's MCP servers with their switch | KEEP | Protects gET lists every installed agent's MCP servers with their switch. Distinct scenario at this file’s stated seam; failure is the wrong caller-visible outcome rather than a collaborator invocation inventory. Baseline oracle evidence: `assert.equal(response.statusCode, 200); assert.deepEqual((response.json() as BoxMcpRunInBoxResponse).servers, [ { name: "github", agent: "claude", runInBox: false }, { name: "postgres", agent: "claude", runInBox: true }, { name: "github", agent: "codex", runInBox: false } ])`. |
| PUT sets one switch and keeps unknown fields | CONSOLIDATE | Surviving persistence owner: round trip sets a switch, preserves unrelated/unknown fields, and now switches it off with a persisted false value. Replaces the parser-only false-acceptance assertion. Baseline oracle evidence: `assert.equal(response.statusCode, 200, response.body); assert.deepEqual( (response.json() as BoxMcpRunInBoxResponse).servers.find((server) => server.agent === "codex"), { name: "github", agent: "codex", runInBox: true } ); assert.equal(stored.extra, 1)`. |
| PUT is refused while the plugin is off and for a bad body | CONSOLIDATE | Surviving public route owner: 409 BOXES_DISABLED and 400 BOX_INVALID_REQUEST for unsupported agent, empty/control-byte name, nonboolean flag and null body; no parser-only seam. Baseline oracle evidence: `assert.equal(refused.statusCode, 409); assert.equal(refused.json().code, "BOXES_DISABLED"); assert.equal(bad.statusCode, 400)`. |
| parseSetRunInBox | CONSOLIDATE | Moved malformed names, nonboolean flags and null into the public PUT route. False flags are now persisted by the existing PUT scenario. Internal parser export removed. Baseline oracle evidence: `assert.deepEqual(parseSetRunInBox({ agent: "grok", name: "x", runInBox: false }), { agent: "grok", name: "x", runInBox: false }); assert.equal(parseSetRunInBox({ agent: "grok", name: "", runInBox: false }), null); assert.equal(parseSetRunInBox({ agent: "grok", name: "a\nb", runInBox: false }), null)`. |

## `apps/daemon/src/boxes/sync/watches.test.ts`

1. **Independent contract:** Design §§8.3, 8.5–8.6, anti-goals; BoxConfigSync/BoxTranscriptSync and tolerant config-change stores.
2. **Recognizable regression:** each row names the distinct failed operation/state/security property; it must fail the listed observable oracle.
3. **Independent oracle:** literal outputs, known native-format fixtures, specified state transitions, or separately observed filesystem/process/network artifacts shown below; no coverage percentage or production-inventory equality is a retention reason.
4. **Stable seam:** codecs for independently specified config formats, real host/box trees, approval routes and sync service.
5. **Refactor resistance:** retained assertions concern accepted inputs, outputs, native protocol bytes or visible side effects; removed private variable/export/call-shape/prose assertions are identified individually.
6. **Unique protection:** codecs own secret target/redaction semantics; service cases own actual cross-tree approval, conflict, path containment and watcher lifecycle; transcript cases own provider resume stores.
**Production callers:** agent launch/turn completion, daemon config watches and approval UI routes. **Risk:** retained native security/persistence/lifecycle scenarios are high consequence; deletions are restricted to the failed bar and named surviving owner. **Validation:** package-directory `TSX_DISABLE_CACHE=1 node --import tsx --test src/boxes/sync/watches.test.ts`; scheduled execution is recorded separately, never inferred from this command.

| Baseline test name | Disposition | Contract, unique failure, and oracle / surviving owner |
|---|---|---|
| keep holds one watch per scope (roots in any order), only for active boxes; release ends a box's watches | REWRITE | Retains externally observed watch subscriptions/release callbacks for dedupe, scope normalization and inactive boxes. Removes duplicated internal watched() counts; no production introspection seam remains. Baseline oracle evidence: `assert.deepEqual(live().sort(), ["b1\|claude\|/h/claude,/p/v1x1", "b1\|claude\|/h/claudex1", "b1\|codex\|/h/codexx1", "b2\|claude\|/h/claudex1"]); assert.equal(watches.watched("b1"), 3); assert.equal(watches.watched("b3"), 0)`. |
| syncing: a successful ensureSynced keeps its scope watched; a failed one does not; turns pass through | REWRITE | Keeps uniquely necessary failed-initial-sync/no-subscription assertion with the intended error reason. Removes wrapper call-shape assertions and successful fake duplicate; actual later-host-edit propagation remains in config-sync.test.ts. Baseline oracle evidence: `assert.deepEqual(ok.live(), ["b1\|grok\|/h/grok,/p/g1x1"]); assert.deepEqual(ok.calls, ["ensureSynced b1\|grok\|/h/grok,/p/g1", "turnEnded b1 t1", "turnStarted b1 t2"]); assert.rejects(other.syncing(failing.sync).ensureSynced("b1", { agent: "claude", configRoots: ["/h"], threadId: null }, new AbortController().signal))`. |
| a watch that cannot start is logged and not held | REWRITE | Replaces introspection/log-count assertions with retry after failed startup and actual live-subscription/release outcomes. Removes the test-only watched() method. Baseline oracle evidence: `assert.equal(watches.watched("b1"), 0); assert.equal(warnings.length, 1)`. |

## `apps/daemon/src/boxes/tooling/install.test.ts`

1. **Independent contract:** Design §10, BoxToolingProvisioner and plugin ToolArtifact/cache/preload contracts.
2. **Recognizable regression:** each row names the distinct failed operation/state/security property; it must fail the listed observable oracle.
3. **Independent oracle:** literal outputs, known native-format fixtures, specified state transitions, or separately observed filesystem/process/network artifacts shown below; no coverage percentage or production-inventory equality is a retention reason.
4. **Stable seam:** real verified archive install/cache/preload with native subprocesses and scripted Windows protocol.
5. **Refactor resistance:** retained assertions concern accepted inputs, outputs, native protocol bytes or visible side effects; removed private variable/export/call-shape/prose assertions are identified individually.
6. **Unique protection:** host plugin installer does not route paths/archives or environment into boxes; these cases uniquely detect per-box install isolation, rollback and preload interoperability.
**Production callers:** box lifecycle and plugin-driven per-session tooling contributions. **Risk:** retained native security/persistence/lifecycle scenarios are high consequence; deletions are restricted to the failed bar and named surviving owner. **Validation:** package-directory `TSX_DISABLE_CACHE=1 node --import tsx --test src/boxes/tooling/install.test.ts`; scheduled execution is recorded separately, never inferred from this command.

| Baseline test name | Disposition | Contract, unique failure, and oracle / surviving owner |
|---|---|---|
| readBoxText reads with the stat's size as its length | KEEP | Protects readBoxText reads with the stat's size as its length. Distinct scenario at this file’s stated seam; failure is the wrong caller-visible outcome rather than a collaborator invocation inventory. Baseline oracle evidence: `assert.equal(await readBoxText(connection, join(ROOT, "version")), "1.2.3\n"); assert.deepEqual(reads, [6]); assert.equal(await readBoxText(connection, join(ROOT, "missing")), null)`. |
| readBoxText refuses a box that sends more than the cap | KEEP | Protects readBoxText refuses a box that sends more than the cap. Distinct scenario at this file’s stated seam; failure is the wrong caller-visible outcome rather than a collaborator invocation inventory. Baseline oracle evidence: `assert.equal(await readBoxText(connection, "/v", 16), null)`. |
